Preparing young learners for exams  
Motivating and supporting learners as they approach exam day  
and onexam day itselfarekey to their success.Here is some  
practical advice to help you do this.  
On exam day  
Look back and reflect  
Allow time for pupils to look back on the exam tasks  
In your last lesson before the exam, remind learners  
they have completed and the progress they are making.  
ofanything theyneed tobring and of the structure

time theexamwill startand howlongitwill last!When

Remind them that they have prepared well for the exam.

Practising exam tasks in class gives pupils a chance to

Inclusive classes  
One in every lO learners is likely to exhibit signs of specific learning difficulties  
(SpLDs). These pages outline some strategies for supporting all learners in  
order to create an inclusive environment in which everyone can thrive.  
Supporting all learners  
Language skills  
When reading, some learners may:  
As we all know, every learner is unique. SpLD learners  
often have strengths, such as holistic thinking, and  
· read more slowly and less accurately.  
problem-solving and visuospatial skills. Individual  
· misread words which can result in misunderstanding.  
strengths should be recognised and used to each  
When listening, some learners may:  
learner's benefit.  
Many of the following suggestions are beneficial for  
· find it challenging to listen to longer texts and  
SpLD learners, and for all learners in your classes.  
remember specific information.  
· find it difficult to distinguish between similar sounds  
Raise self-esteem and self-confidence  
in English or sounds that are different from the  
Regular praise and acknowledgement of learners'  
sound system of their first language.  
work and effort are crucial. Setting short-term,  
When writing, some learners may:  
achievable goals and rewarding learners for reaching  
them are great ways of boosting self-confidence and  
· find it particularly hard to spell in English.  
self-esteem.  
· have difficulties organising ideas.  
· find handwriting challenging.  
Create an inclusive environment  
·make more grammatical errors.  
· If possible, speak with learners and their parents  
to establish what adjustments and assistance they  
·be less skilled at reviewing their work.  
might find useful.  
When speaking, some learners may:  
· Assess progress regularly and make adjustments to  
· find it challenging to express their ideas coherently.  
learning goals and instructional methods.  
· struggle to retrieve words quickly from memory  
·Assesslearning outcomesfairly:focuson more than  
under the pressure of oral communication.

· have difficulty paying attention to accuracy and  
theseareareasthatsomelearnerswithSpLDsfind

content at the same time, when either speaking or  
writing.  
Practical tips  
·MakeallowancesforlearnerswithSpLDswhowork  
more slowly and need extra time to complete tasks.  
· Give learners extended time and additional breaks  
to complete assessment tasks.  
· Give learners the opportunity to work (or  
participate) in a variety of groupand pairactivities,  
in roles based on their strengths.  
· Organise peer support for learners.

strategies

and revising these predictions;

A key element of these programmes is the development

## 第 18 页

Learner preferences  
Research and experience show that pupils have different needs when it  
comes to learning English. There will probably be a mixture of levels in  
your classroom, with some learners needing more support or perhaps  
more time to complete an activity.  
Extra support and challenges  
The Teacher's Book is full of suggestions on how

Vocabulary and language are reinforced and recycled:  
pupils have multiple opportunities to put them into  
practice through songs, chants and games, as well as  
through traditional activities. This approach also helps  
improve memory and retention as content is presented  
and practised indifferentmodesandcontexts.

are ... Pupil B responds by drawing a tick or a cross in the air. Then  
they swap. Check for correct use of the verbs, articles (a/an) and  
plural forms.  
Page 100  
·Consolidation activity: My favourite pet  
Pupils each draw their favourite pet (real orimaginary). They give  
the pet a name and write a sentence about it. Set a time limit.Pupils  
then mingle and ask each other about their pet:  
A: Have you got a pet?  
B: Yes, I've got a ...  
B: It's ...  
A:What's yourpet's name?  
Page 101  
·Consolidation activity: Activities in a park  
Pupils work in pairs. They think of five activities they like to do in a  
park. They join up with another pair. Pairs mime their activities for  
the other pair to guess.  
Page 102  
· Consolidation activity: Ask me a question  
Materials:Kid'sBox2flashcardsofStartersexamwords  
Display four flashcards on the board, numbered 1-4 (one food item,  
one toy, one clothing item, one school item) from the Starters exam  
word list. In pairs, pupils ask a question about each item. Write  
question prompts on the board, e.g. What's your favourite ...? Have  
you got...? Do you like...? Repeat with different words.  
T124Consolidation andExtension activities
